PSI COMP80 · the 3 KB ROM · 0000–0BFF

COMP80 ROM Listings

Annotated listings of the PSI COMP80's ROMs: the monitor that every ROM starts with, and the three languages that can sit above it in the remaining 2 KB. BURP is the original, from the Wireless World articles; FORTH and LISP were written for this project. Every line shows its address and the bytes it assembles to, read from the ROM images, and every name or branch address is a link to the line it means.

See also the screenshots, the LISP page and the emulator, which runs all three ROMs.

0000–03FFMonitor ROM, 1 KB
0400–0BFFBURP, FORTH or LISP, 2 KB
0C00–1FFFRAM, 5 KB
8000–87FFScreen, 64 × 32
MonitorBURPFORTHLISPHex dumps
0000–03FF

Monitor

The first kilobyte of every COMP80 ROM, the same in all three. Reset clears the screen and prints READY. Keys arrive as non-maskable interrupts: the handler at 0066 echoes each key on the screen, and a command key turns into a jump to its command. The commands examine, change, move, fill, find and list memory, read and write tape, and run code; RS (the ^ key) enters the language at 0800.

An annotated disassembly. The names and comments come from the earlier disassemblies (now in old/), corrected where the bytes disagreed. Source: rom/psicomp80.asm.

It assembles with z80asm to psicomp80.rom byte for                   ; byte (rom/build.sh checks). Each line's comment starts with the address and                   ; the bytes, as in the magazine's hex listing. Branch targets without a                   ; name are labelled L<address>.                   ;                   ; Made from the earlier annotated disassemblies (old/) by                   ; rom/tools/make_rom_asm.py; edit this file from now on.                    ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; PSI COMP-80 ROM DISASSEMBLY (Combined)                   ; Updated: 2026-02-03  (added annotations for $06BB/token $26; notes on $054B/$065F/$06AB)                   ;                   ; 0000-03FF: Monitor (from comp80_monitor_0000_03FF_annotated.asm)                   ; 0400-0BFF: BURP (derived from ROM_listing_fixedup.txt + trace annotations)                   ;                   ; NOTE: BURP section includes trace-driven path map for RUN 5 and monitor-call annotations.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                    ; ===============================                   ; MONITOR ROM 0000-03FF                   ; ===============================                    ; PSI COMP80 Monitor (first 1KB of ROM) - annotated disassembly                   ; ORG 0000h (monitor mapped at 0x0000 at reset)                   ;                   ; Keyboard notes:                   ;   IN A,($01) reads the latched key byte (active-low), then CPL inverts it.                   ;   CP A,1Eh / JP Z,0800h implements the RS key (ASCII 0x1E, Record Separator) to enter BURP.                   ;                   ; ------------------------------------------------------------                   ; Key/Control-code conventions observed in this monitor ROM                   ;                   ; The keyboard hardware delivers an active-low keycode on port $01.                   ; The monitor inverts it (CPL) and then normalises certain values:                   ;   - 0x0D (CR/ENTER) is remapped to 0x1F (US) as the monitor’s                   ;     internal end-of-line marker.                   ;   - For codes >= 0x40, the monitor ANDs with 0x1F, effectively                   ;     folding uppercase letters to 1..26 (A=1, B=2, ... Z=26).                   ;     This means command keywords like ALPH/LIST/etc are NOT stored                   ;     as ASCII strings in ROM; they’re matched using these 5-bit codes.                   ;                   ; Control characters used as special keys:                   ;   0x1C  FS  (File Separator)  - acts as an escape back to command state                   ;         (Monitor docs mention: in ALT mode, type FS to return to commands).                   ;   0x1D  GS  (Group Separator) - used as a delimiter/terminator in some input loops.                   ;   0x1E  RS  (Record Separator)- ‘RS’ key to enter BURP (JP 0800h).                   ;   0x1F  US  (Unit Separator)  - used internally as ‘end of line’ (remapped from CR).                   ; ------------------------------------------------------------                    ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; Monitor command dispatch (as observed in ROM)                   ; The monitor reads a normalised command selector in A from CALL $0355 and                   ; compares it against a small set of command IDs, then jumps to handlers.                   ; These IDs are *not* ASCII; they are compact key/command codes produced by                   ; the monitor's command scanner.                   ;                   ; Mappings below are inferred from the handler behaviour and monitor_cmds.md:                   ;   $FC : RUN  XXXX     (parse address -> JP (HL))        ()                   ;   $13 : ALT           (alter memory; loops until FS/RESET)                   ;   $0F : COR           (scan/replace AA->BB across range)                   ;   $FE : FIND          (search for consecutive bytes XX YY)                   ;   $08 : LIST          (list memory to screen)                   ;   $04 : PRINT         (list memory on second output device)                   ;   $F1 : TAPE          (write block to tape/serial on port $20)                   ;   $F2 : READ          (read tape into memory via port $00)                   ;   $07 : ALPH          (VDU alphanumeric test pattern)                   ;   $FD : PROM          (program $1C00-$1CFF via ports)  ()                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; === v5: relabelled command dispatch + monitor commands ===                    CMD_RUN_CODE:   EQU $FC                   CMD_LOAD_CODE:  EQU $F8                   CMD_MOV_CODE:   EQU $09                   CMD_FILL_CODE:  EQU $06                   CMD_PROM_CODE:  EQU $FD                   CMD_ALT_CODE:   EQU $13                   CMD_COR_CODE:   EQU $0F                   CMD_FIND_CODE:  EQU $FE                   CMD_LIST_CODE:  EQU $08                   CMD_PRINT_CODE: EQU $04                   CMD_TAPE_CODE:  EQU $F1                   CMD_READ_CODE:  EQU $F2                   CMD_ALPH_CODE:  EQU $07                            ORG     $0000 0000               MON_START:0000  31 DF 1F             LD SP,$1FDF           0003  11 00 80             LD DE,$8000           0006  CD CE 03             CALL PRINT_INLINE     ; Print inline string (see STR_PRINT_HL_AT_RETADDR)                   ; Inline data consumed by CALL $03CE (03CE uses EX (SP),HL to treat the return address as a string pointer,                   ; prints bytes until terminator $1D (GS), then returns to the byte *after* the terminator).0009               READY_PROMPT_STR:0009  20 12 05 01          DB $20,$12,$05,$01,$04,$19,$1D   ; " READY" using 5-bit letter codes (R=12h,E=05h,A=01h,D=04h,Y=19h), terminated by GS ($1D)000D  04 19 1D     0010  CD C4 03             CALL PAD_TO_NEXT_LINE ; pad after READY prompt (advance cursor to start of next 64-col line)0013  3E 80                LD A,$80              0015  D3 00                OUT ($00),A                              ; --- Main key dispatch (cold-start / command-loop entry)                   ; CALL $0355 returns a keycode in A (already normalised elsewhere).                   ; The monitor compares A against a small set of non-ASCII command keycodes                   ; and branches to the relevant handler.                    ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; COMMAND_LOOP: wait for a command selector, dispatch to handlers                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0017  CD 55 03             CALL GET_KEY_SELECTOR ; READ_COMMAND_ID -> A                   ; A == $FC : special command key (non-ASCII)                   ; A == $FC : RUN XXXX (jump to address) 001A  FE FC                CP $FC                ; RUN XXXX001C  20 0D                JR NZ,L002B           001E  18 22                JR L0042              0020  F5                   PUSH AF               0021  3E 20                LD A,$20              0023  12                   LD (DE),A             0024  13                   INC DE                0025  F1                   POP AF                0026  C9                   RET                   0027  FF                   RST $38               0028  C3 AA 01             JP L01AA                                 ; A == $06 : FILL XXXX (fill from XXXX to next xx00 with FF)                   ;                    ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; CMD_FILL: implements FILL XXXX (fill from XXXX to next xx00 with FF)                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------002B               L002B:002B  FE 06                CP $06                ; FILL XXXX (fill to page with FF)002D  20 1A                JR NZ,L0049           002F  CD DE 03             CALL READ_HEX_WORD    ; FILL: read start address XXXX -> HL0032               L0032:0032  36 FF                LD (HL),$FF           0034  2C                   INC L                 0035  20 FB                JR NZ,L0032           0037  C7                   RST $00                                  ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; Interrupt handler for the MM57109 stores values in the address pointed to                   ; via the HL register, which should have been setup prior to interrups being                   ; enabled.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0038  F5                   PUSH AF               0039  DB 03                IN A,($03)            003B  E6 0F                AND $0F               003D  77                   LD (HL),A             003E  23                   INC HL                003F  F1                   POP AF                0040  FB                   EI                    0041  C9                   RET                   0042               L0042:0042  CD 93 03             CALL CLEAR_SCREEN     ; clear screen/work area before showing READY / accepting command0045  CD DE 03             CALL READ_HEX_WORD    ; RUN: read target address XXXX -> HL (then JP (HL))0048  E9                   JP (HL)                                  ; A == $09 : MOV XXXXYYYYZZZZ (block move)                   ;                    ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; CMD_MOV: implements MOV XXXX YYYY ZZZZ (LDIR block move)                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0049               L0049:0049  FE 09                CP $09                ; MOV XXXX YYYY ZZZZ (block move)004B  20 48                JR NZ,L0095           004D  CD DE 03             CALL READ_HEX_WORD    ; MOV: read source address XXXX -> HL0050  E5                   PUSH HL               0051  E7                   RST $20               0052  CD DE 03             CALL READ_HEX_WORD    ; MOV: read destination address YYYY -> HL0055  C1                   POP BC                0056  C5                   PUSH BC               0057  A7                   AND A                 0058  ED 42                SBC HL,BC             005A  E5                   PUSH HL               005B  C1                   POP BC                005C  E7                   RST $20               005D  CD DE 03             CALL READ_HEX_WORD    ; MOV: read length/limit ZZZZ -> HL (see handler logic)0060  D1                   POP DE                0061  EB                   EX DE,HL              0062  ED B0                LDIR                  0064               L0064:0064  C7                   RST $00               0065  FF                   RST $38                                  ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; NMI (keyboard 'data ready') handler at 0066h                   ;                   ; Key idea: the COMP-80 generates an NMI on any keyboard event. The monitor *expects*                   ; this NMI to occur only while it is deliberately waiting in a HALT instruction.                   ;                   ; On NMI entry, the Z80 pushes the return address (PC) to the stack. This handler                   ; swaps that return address into HL (EX (SP),HL), backs up one byte, and checks the                   ; opcode that was interrupted. If it wasn't HALT (0x76), it treats this as an                   ; unexpected key interrupt and resets back to 0000h.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0066               NMI_KBD_ISR:0066  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; HL := return address (PC) from NMI entry0067  2B                   DEC HL                ; point at opcode that was interrupted0068  7E                   LD A,(HL)             ; fetch interrupted opcode0069  FE 76                CP $76                ; was it HALT?006B  20 93                JR NZ,MON_START       ; no: unexpected NMI -> reset monitor006D  23                   INC HL                ; yes: restore return address006E  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; put return address back on stack006F               KBD_READ_PORT01:006F  DB 01                IN A,($01)            ; read raw keycode from keyboard latch (port $01)0071  2F                   CPL                   ; invert active-low keycode0072  FE 0D                CP $0D                ; CR/ENTER from keyboard?0074  20 02                JR NZ,L0078           0076  3E 1F                LD A,$1F              ; remap CR -> US (0x1F) as end-of-line marker0078               L0078:0078  FE 40                CP $40                007A  38 02                JR C,L007E            007C  E6 1F                AND $1F               ; fold 0x40+ range to 5-bit (letters: A=1..Z=26)007E               L007E:007E  FE 1C                CP $1C                ; FS (0x1C) - escape/return to command state0080  28 E2                JR Z,L0064            0082               L0082:0082  FE 1E                CP $1E                0084               BURP_ENTRY_JP:0084  CA 00 08             JP Z,BURP_ENTRY       ;                      ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; INLINE_DELAY: cycle-burn delay with 1-byte inline parameter                   ;                   ; Call pattern:                   ;     CALL INLINE_DELAY                   ;     DB   n          ; delay count (burn cycles roughly proportional to n)                   ;                   ; Implementation trick (same family as 03CE):                   ;   EX (SP),HL swaps the caller's return address into HL. That return address                   ;   points at the inline byte 'n'. The routine reads (HL) into B, then runs a                   ;   tight cycle-wasting loop (EX (SP),HL repeated) for B iterations.                   ;   Finally it increments HL to point past the inline byte and swaps that                   ;   updated HL back onto the stack as the return address, so RET continues                   ;   at the instruction after the DB parameter.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0336               INLINE_DELAY_1B:0336  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; HL = return address (points to inline delay count byte)0337  C5                   PUSH BC               ; preserve BC0338  46                   LD B,(HL)             ; B = inline delay count0339               L0339:0339  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; waste cycles (part of delay loop)033A  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; waste cycles033B  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; waste cycles033C  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; waste cycles033D  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; waste cycles033E  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; waste cycles033F  10 F8                DJNZ L0339            ; loop B times0341  C1                   POP BC                ; restore BC0342  23                   INC HL                ; HL -> byte after inline parameter0343  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; update return address on stack to skip parameter0344  C9                   RET                                      ;                   ; Clears the top line and sets DE to 8008                   ;0345               L0345:0345  11 08 80             LD DE,$8008           0348  D5                   PUSH DE               0349  CD C4 03             CALL PAD_TO_NEXT_LINE ; align output cursor (start-of-line) before status/echo output034C  D1                   POP DE                034D  C9                   RET                                      ;                   ; Used to format results as in FIND and COR this rounds DE up to the next multiple of 8                   ;034E               DE_TO_NEXT_8:034E  7B                   LD A,E                034F  E6 07                AND $07               0351  C8                   RET Z                 0352  E7                   RST $20               0353  18 F9                JR DE_TO_NEXT_8                          ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; $0355: GETKEY/GETCMD – wait for a keypress using HALT+NMI, then decode it                   ;                   ; The COMP-80 monitor uses the Z80 NMI (vector $0066) as its keyboard interrupt.                   ; The mainline executes HALT to sleep until an NMI arrives. The NMI handler:                   ;   * verifies the CPU really was halted (checks the interrupted opcode == $76)                   ;     and jumps to $0000 (cold monitor restart) if not halted                   ;   * reads the keyboard latch via IN A,($01) / CPL and normalises the value                   ;     (letters folded to 1..26 via AND $1F, CR remapped, etc.)                   ;   * returns with RETN, leaving the decoded key value in A                   ;                   ; This routine ($0355) then performs a small decode/debounce sequence:                   ;   1) Sample 1: HALT -> A, take low 5 bits (A AND $1F) into B                   ;   2) Writes that sample to $8010 (scratch/display state)                   ;   3) Sample 2: HALT -> A, take low 5 bits into C                   ;   4) Waits until the key returns to idle (A == $20, SPACE) before completing                   ;   5) Returns A = (C - B).  This appears to be the monitor's compact keycode                   ;      decode step (two successive samples combined via subtraction).                   ;                   ; NOTE: The exact meaning of B and C depends on the keyboard encoder; empirically                   ; the final A is the command selector used by the dispatch compares near $0080.                   ; --------------------------------------------------------------------------- 0355               GET_KEY_SELECTOR:0355  C5                   PUSH BC               0356  76                   HALT                  0357  E6 1F                AND $1F               0359  47                   LD B,A                035A  11 01 80             LD DE,$8001           035D  CD C4 03             CALL PAD_TO_NEXT_LINE ; align output cursor before writing prompt/status area0360  1E 10                LD E,$10              0362  78                   LD A,B                0363  12                   LD (DE),A             0364  13                   INC DE                0365  76                   HALT                  0366               L0366:0366  E6 1F                AND $1F               0368  4F                   LD C,A                0369  76                   HALT                  036A  FE 20                CP $20                036C  20 F8                JR NZ,L0366           036E  79                   LD A,C                036F  90                   SUB B                 0370  C1                   POP BC                0371  C9                   RET                                      ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; LIST_ADVANCE_AND_MAYBE_NEWLINE                   ;                   ; Called after outputting a byte in the LIST/PRINT-style memory display.                   ; - Always outputs at least one space (RST 20h writes ' ' to (DE) and INC DE).                   ; - Adds extra spacing every 4 bytes (when (L & 3) == 0).                   ; - When a 16-byte group is complete (when (L & 0x0F) == 0), it pads to the                   ;   next 64-column line boundary and then jumps into the address-print path                   ;   at 039F to print the new line's address (HL) followed by the next bytes.                   ;                   ; Uses L (low byte of current address) to decide grouping/wrap points.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0372               LIST_ADVANCE_AND_MAYBE_NEWLINE:0372  E7                   RST $20               0373  7D                   LD A,L                0374  E6 03                AND $03               0376  20 02                JR NZ,L037A           0378  E7                   RST $20               0379  E7                   RST $20               037A               L037A:037A  7D                   LD A,L                037B  E6 0F                AND $0F               037D  C0                   RET NZ                037E  CD C4 03             CALL PAD_TO_NEXT_LINE ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; CLEAR_SCREEN_8040_87FF                   ;                   ; Clears the monitor's display/work area by filling 0x8040..0x87FF with SPACE                   ; (0x20).  Uses RST 20h which writes a space to (DE) and increments DE.                   ;                   ; On entry : DE preserved                   ; On exit  : 0x8040..0x87FF = 0x20, DE preserved                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------0393               CLEAR_SCREEN:0393  D5                   PUSH DE               0394  11 40 80             LD DE,$8040           0397               L0397:0397  E7                   RST $20               0398  7A                   LD A,D                0399  FE 88                CP $88                039B  20 FA                JR NZ,L0397           039D  D1                   POP DE                039E  C9                   RET                                      ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; PRINT_HEX_WORD_HL  (entry at 039F)                   ;                   ; Purpose:                   ;   Emit the 16-bit value in HL as 4 hexadecimal characters at (DE), advancing DE.                   ;   Output format: HHLL plus a trailing SPACE (via RST 20h) to separate fields.                   ;                   ; How it works:                   ;   - Load A=H and call PRINT_HEX_BYTE (03AA) to output two hex chars.                   ;   - Load A=L and call PRINT_HEX_BYTE (03AA) to output two hex chars.                   ;   - Output a trailing SPACE via RST 20h (which writes 0x20 at (DE) and INC DE).                   ;                   ; Used by:                   ;   - LIST/PRINT style routines to print the current address at the start of each line.                   ;   - FIND/COR/etc when reporting addresses.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------039F               PRINT_HEX_WORD_HL:039F  7C                   LD A,H                03A0  CD AA 03             CALL PRINT_HEX_BYTE   03A3  7D                   LD A,L                03A4  CD AA 03             CALL PRINT_HEX_BYTE   03A7  E7                   RST $20               03A8  C9                   RET                                      ; --- Hex output helpers -----------------------------------------------------                   ; $03A9: PRINT_HEX_BYTE_FROM_HL                   ;   Loads A=(HL) and falls through into $03AA to emit two hex chars to (DE).                   ;   Used by the LIST/memory-dump code above.                   ; $03AA: PRINT_HEX_BYTE (A)  ; emits high nibble then low nibble                   ; $03B9: NIBBLE_TO_ASCII     ; converts 0..15 into '0'..'9','A'..'F'                   ; ------------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; PAD_TO_NEXT_LINE_64COL  (entry at 03C4)                   ;                   ; Purpose:                   ;   Pad output with ASCII spaces until the display cursor DE reaches the next                   ;   64-column boundary (i.e. E & 0x3F == 0). This effectively advances to the                   ;   start of the next screen line on the COMP-80 (64 chars/row).                   ;                   ; How it works:                   ;   - Uses RST 20h to output a SPACE at (DE) and increment DE.                   ;   - Loops until (E & 0x3F) == 0, then exits via the common epilogue at 03DB.                   ;   - Prologue EX (SP),HL preserves caller HL using the same stack trick as the                   ;     inline string printer at 03CE (shared epilogue).                   ;03C4               PAD_TO_NEXT_LINE:03C4  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; preserve caller HL via return-address swap (shared epilogue pattern)03C5  F5                   PUSH AF               ; save AF (A used for column check / clobbered by output)03C6               L03C6:03C6  7B                   LD A,E                ; A = current column (low byte of cursor)03C7  E6 3F                AND $3F               ; mask to 0..63 (column within 64-char row)03C9  28 10                JR Z,L03DB            ; if already at col 0, exit via epilogue at 03DB03CB  E7                   RST $20               ; output SPACE at (DE), increment DE (see RST 20h)03CC  18 F8                JR L03C6              ; loop until aligned                   ; ------------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; STR_PRINT_INLINE_AT_RETADDR  (entry at 03CE)                   ;                   ; Purpose:                   ;   Print an inline, terminator-delimited string placed immediately after the CALL                   ;   instruction. This is how the monitor prints the READY prompt at reset.                   ;                   ; Calling convention (Z80 trick):                   ;   Caller does:   CALL 03CE                   ;                 DB <byte0>,<byte1>,...,<terminator>                   ;   On entry, the return address (address of the first DB byte) is on the stack.                   ;   EX (SP),HL swaps that return address into HL, giving HL = string pointer.                   ;   When printing finishes, EX (SP),HL puts the *updated* HL back on the stack,                   ;   so RET returns to the byte *after* the terminator.                   ;                   ; String format:                   ;   Bytes are COMP-80 keyboard/display codes (often 5-bit letters: A=01..Z=1A,                   ;   plus space=20h). Terminator is GS = 1Dh.                   ;                   ; Output:                   ;   Each byte is sent to the normal character output routine at 0082.                   ; ------------------------------------------------------------------------------03CE               PRINT_INLINE:03CE  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; HL <- return address (string ptr), [SP] <- old HL03CF  F5                   PUSH AF               ; preserve A across printing03D0               L03D0:03D0  7E                   LD A,(HL)             ; fetch next byte of inline string03D1  23                   INC HL                ; advance pointer03D2  FE 1D                CP $1D                ; terminator? (GS = 1Dh)03D4  28 05                JR Z,L03DB            ; yes -> finish (return address now points past terminator)03D6  CD 82 00             CALL L0082            ; output this character03D9  18 F5                JR L03D0              ; loop for next byte03DB               L03DB:03DB  F1                   POP AF                ; restore A03DC  E3                   EX (SP),HL            ; put updated HL back as return address; restore original HL03DD  C9                   RET                   ; returns to byte after terminator in caller stream                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; HEX INPUT HELPERS                   ;                   ; 03DE  HEXWORD(): read 4 hex digits from the keyboard and return the 16-bit                   ;       value in HL (high byte first). Used by most monitor commands that take                   ;       addresses/ranges: LIST, RUN, LOAD, MOV, FILL, ALT, COR, TAPE, READ, ...                   ;                   ;       Implementation: calls HEXBYTE() twice:                   ;           H <- HEXBYTE()   (two hex digits)                   ;           L <- HEXBYTE()   (two hex digits)                   ;                   ; 03E7  HEXBYTE(): read 2 hex digits and return the byte in A.                   ;       Implementation: reads two nibbles via HEXDIGIT(), shifts the first                   ;       left by 4, then adds the second.                   ;                   ; 03F6  HEXDIGIT(): wait for a keypress (HALT; NMI delivers keycode in A),                   ;       then normalise into a 0..15 nibble:                   ;         - if A < 0x20 (digit-ish path), add 9 so that A..F map correctly                   ;         - mask with 0x0F                   ;                   ; NOTE: Key acquisition is interrupt-driven. The mainline HALTs, the NMI handler                   ;       (at 0066) verifies we were halted (opcode 0x76) and then reads port 01,                   ;       inverts it (CPL), normalises it, and returns with A holding the key code.                   ; ---------------------------------------------------------------------------03DE               READ_HEX_WORD:03DE  CD E7 03             CALL READ_HEX_BYTE    ; Read 16-bit hex word (4 digits) into HL  (HEXBYTE: read 2 hex digits -> A)03E1  67                   LD H,A                03E2  CD E7 03             CALL READ_HEX_BYTE    ; HEXBYTE: read 2 hex digits -> A03E5  6F                   LD L,A                03E6  C9                   RET                                      ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; 03E7  HEXBYTE_FROM_KEYPAD                   ;                   ; Reads a single byte expressed as two hexadecimal digits from the keyboard.                   ; Returns:                   ;   A = byte value (00h..FFh)                   ; Notes:                   ;   - Uses HALT+NMI keyboard delivery (see GET_KEY_SELECTOR / NMI handler).                   ;   - Calls HEXDIGIT (03F6) twice to obtain two nibbles (0..15),                   ;     shifts the first nibble left by 4, then adds the second.                   ;   - Used by HEXWORD (03DE) and by commands that accept 8-bit parameters.                   ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------03E7               READ_HEX_BYTE:03E7  CD F6 03             CALL READ_HEX_DIGIT   ; HEXBYTE: read 2 hex digits -> A03EA               L03EA:03EA  C5                   PUSH BC               03EB  07                   RLCA                  03EC  07                   RLCA                  03ED  07                   RLCA                  03EE  07                   RLCA                  03EF  47                   LD B,A                03F0  CD F6 03             CALL READ_HEX_DIGIT   03F3  80                   ADD A,B               ; combine low nibble with (high_nibble<<4)03F4  C1                   POP BC                03F5  C9                   RET                                      ;                   ; Reads a single keyboard character end if a letter adds 6 then truncates to 4 bit binary (used as part of 03E7)                   ;03F6               READ_HEX_DIGIT:03F6  76                   HALT                  03F7               L03F7:03F7  FE 20                CP $20                03F9  30 02                JR NC,L03FD           03FB  C6 09                ADD A,$09             03FD               L03FD:03FD  E6 0F                AND $0F               03FF  C9                   RET                                      ; =============================================================================                   ; Added in v16 (based on v15)                   ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; 0383  LIST_PAD_TO_START_BYTE_COLUMN                   ;                   ; Purpose:                   ;   When a LIST-style dump begins at an address not aligned to a 16-byte                   ;   boundary, this routine outputs the correct number of SPACE characters so                   ;   that the *first printed data byte* appears under the proper column.                   ;                   ; Operation (conceptual):                   ;   i = L & 0x0F          ; byte index within a 16-byte line                   ;   spaces =                   ;       3*i               ; 'HH ' per byte (two hex chars + space)                   ;     + 2*(i/4)           ; extra group spacing every 4 bytes                   ;                   ;   Outputs that many spaces using RST 20h, advancing DE accordingly.                   ;                   ; Why this exists:                   ;   LIST output groups bytes in sets of four with extra spacing. If LIST                   ;   starts at (say) xxxx3, the address header is printed first, then this                   ;   routine pads so the first byte printed lines up under the correct column.                   ;                   ; Call sites (from v15):                   ;   - 00A6 : align listing output before printing first data byte                   ;   - 0326 : LIST command, align for unaligned start address                   ;                   ; =============================================================================
0400–0BFF

BURP

The language in the original ROM, from the Wireless World articles: a small BASIC-like language whose arithmetic is done by a National MM57109 calculator chip. The Z80 sends the chip its key codes (MM_SEND) and reads back the digits of the result (MM_OUT_X). Entry is BURP_ENTRY; from 0BB5 the ROM holds the articles' sample program.

An annotated disassembly, in the same file as the monitor: rom/psicomp80.asm.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; MM57109_SEND_INSTRUCTION_IN_A ($042E)                   ;                   ;  Purpose:                   ;    Executes the MM57109 instruction in register A. The sequence checks that the MM57109 is ready, outputs                   ;    the instruction with hold off, waits for READY to go off (to indicate instruction is being execited) and then puts the hold on again,                   ;    this stops the MM57109 after the current instuction has been completed.                   ;                   ;  Calling convention (inferred from ROM usage):                   ;    IN : A  = byte to send (command/data)                   ;    OUT: A  preserved (via B), flags not guaranteed                   ;    Uses: B  (saves A in B), polls port $03 bit5 (mask $20)                   ;    Preserves: BC (PUSH/POP), other regs unaffected                   ;                   ;  Hardware behaviour (as used by ROM):                   ;    - Port $03 bit5 (mask $20) is treated as RDY/handshake status.                   ;    - Port $60 is the output latch to the coprocessor interface.                   ;    - Protocol implemented here:                   ;        1) Wait for RDY=0 (busy/ready-to-accept phase)                   ;        2) Output the byte (A) to port $60                   ;        3) Wait for RDY=1 (acknowledge phase)                   ;        4) Output the same byte again with bit7 set (A|$80) to complete strobe                   ;                   ;  Notes:                   ;    - This is the primary low-level primitive used throughout BURP to talk to                   ;      the MM57109 (directly or via wrapper routines like $0446/$0460).                   ;    - If port $03 bit5 behaviour is wrong in emulation, BURP can hang in the                   ;      busy/ack loops here or mis-sequence the coprocessor stream.                   ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; — Z80 polls port 0x03 bit5 (RDY), OUT to 0x60, waits RDY=1 then sets bit7 and OUT again (MM57109 hold).042E               MM_SEND:042E  C5                   PUSH BC               ; preserve caller's BC042F  47                   LD B,A                ; B := A (save byte to send)                   ; Is MM57109 Ready?0430               L0430:0430  DB 03                IN A,($03)            ; poll status: IN A,($03), is the MM57109 Ready?0432  E6 20                AND $20               ; mask RDY bit (bit5)0434  20 FA                JR NZ,L0430           ; wait for READY state, busy is indicated by RDY==1, and ready is indicated by RDY=0                   ; Send instruction to MM571090436  78                   LD A,B                ; restore byte to send0437  D3 60                OUT ($60),A           ; phase 1: OUT ($60),A                   ; Wait for MM57109 to show it is executing the instruction (RDY=1 when it is busy executing, ie not ready)0439               L0439:0439  DB 03                IN A,($03)            ; poll for acknowledge: IN A,($03)043B  E6 20                AND $20               ; mask RDY bit (bit5)043D  28 FA                JR Z,L0439            ; wait for RDY==1 (indication that the instruction is being executed)                   ; Set the hold bit. This holds the MM57109 when it has completed the current instruction (ie when RDY=0)043F  78                   LD A,B                ; restore byte to send0440  F6 80                OR $80                ; set hold bit70442  D3 60                OUT ($60),A           ; Sets HOLD0444  C1                   POP BC                ; Restore BC0445  C9                   RET                   ; RET                   ; ## BURP_SEND_INLINE_MM57109_INSTRUCTIONS_UNTIL_FF ($0446)                    ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; BURP_SEND_INLINE_MM57109_INSTRUCTIONS_UNTIL_FF ($0446)                   ;             RET                                      ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; BURP_EXPR_FINISH_NUMBER_LITERAL_AND_EMIT_16B BURP_EXPR_FINISH_NUMBER_LITERAL_AND_EMIT_16B ($0577)                   ;  Tail routine used after parsing a number literal (and also by the 'single-token'                   ;  small-token path) to emit a fixed-width 16-byte operand block to the MM57109 stream.                   ;                   ;  Behaviour (observed):                   ;    - Restores HL/DE after lookahead parsing     RET                                      ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; MM57109_SEND_AND_WAIT_RDDY_BR ($058A)                   ;  Handles the MM57109 BR (branch) output which pulses low whenever one of the MM57109                   ;  branch instructions proves to be true.                   ;  Example instuctions are (passed in register A) are                   ;                   ;      TJC*   0x10(o20) - Test Jump Conditional                   ;      TX=0*  0x11(o21) - Test X=0                   ;      TXLTO* 0x12(o22) - Test X < 0                   ;      TXF*   0x13(o23) - Test |X| < 1 i.e. test if x is a fraction                   ;      TERR*  0x14(o24) - Test error flag                   ;                   ;  The subroutine starts the execution of the instruction in resgister A and then reads in                   ;  the 6 bit data word from the MM57109. The 4 digit out lines are blanked odd so that                   ;  only the READY and BR lines both initally high get through (0591). By continually reading                   ;  and jumping back on even parity the Z80 is effectively waiting for one of these two lines to become active.                   ;  If it is the BR line the Z80 outputs a NOP to the 57109 (because when tests prove true the 57109                   ;  immediately looks for a new instruction and waits for its completion).                   ;  If READY became active to indicate a failed test the last procedure is jumped.                   ;  Finally the read in and masked byte which caused the exit from the parity check loop stored in register B                   ;  as part of that loop is read into A and masked for bit 6 so that the state of the line and hence the zero flag in the F register is                   ;  set on a successfil test.                   ;                   ;  Send a byte in A reg to the MM57109, then wait for a status condition on port $03, returns                   ;                   ;                   ;  Sequence (inferred from hardware description + code):                   ;    1) Calls MM57109_SEND_INSTRUCTION_IN_A(A=byte) to output the command/data.                   ;    2) Polls port $03 and examines bits $20 (RDY) and $40 (BR/test).                   ;       Uses a parity trick on (A & $60) to wait until exactly ONE of the two                   ;       status bits is asserted (i.e., value is $20 or $40; not $00 or $60).                   ;    3) If the asserted bit is RDY ($20), outputs $3F to port $60 and waits for                   ;       RDY to deassert (RDY==0).                   ;    4) Returns A = latched status bit6 (A & $40) from the sample taken.                   ;       (Z flag set if bit6 clear; NZ if bit6 set).                   ;                   ;  Calling convention:                   ;    IN : A = byte to send                   ;    OUT: A = $00 or $40 (bit6 status); flags reflect A (Z/NZ)                   ;    Preserves: BC (push/pop), restores DE/HL unchanged                   ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------058A               MM_SEND_TEST:058A  C5                   PUSH BC               ; PUSH BC (preserve)058B  CD 2E 04             CALL MM_SEND          ; In the                   ; context of BURP parsing, these behave like "consume next token/char into A"                   ; checkpoints.  On real hardware the system is interrupt-driven, so HALT is used                   ; as a cheap synchronisation point while an interrupt service routine supplies                   ; the next input token/character.  If your ROM listing has any ambiguity around                   ; $76 bytes, these are the *first* ones to re-check on the original printout.                   ;                   ; Data layout (inferred within the selected 16-byte block):                   ;   Offsets are relative to the base address produced by $07AC / initialised by $0714.                   ;   - $+00..$+09 : 10-char ASCII field being built (right-justified), padded with ' '                   ;   - $+0A..$+0E : padding/flags area (initialised to $A0 later)                   ;   - $+2A..$+2D : optional extra 3-byte suffix copied out if block+$2A == 05                   ;     (Note: this implies the "block" can be a larger structure than 16 bytes;                   ;      $07AC seems to return a base inside a wider workspace region.)                   ;                   ; Behaviour summary:                   ;   1) Initialises the operand/format workspace (CALL $0714).                   ;   2) Parses an optional leading '-' and/or '.' and a run of digit-like tokens                   ;      (>= $28, digit encoded in low nibble) into the field buffer.                   ;   3) Normalises / shifts digits to insert a decimal point '.' (0x2E) when needed:                   ;      - If it detects an exponent or specific prefix pattern, it shifts right/left                   ;        and inserts '.' at a fixed position.                   ;   4) Pads the tail of the field with spaces to a fixed width.                   ;   5) Copies the resulting 10-char field to the output buffer at DE.                   ;   6) Handles separators/terminators:                   ;      - If next input is ',' then it may emit additional items                   ;      - It expects $1F as an end-of-line/statement sentinel (via RST $20 checks)                   ;   7) Optionally appends a 3-byte suffix from offset $2B..$2D when offset $2A == 05.                   ;                   ; Side-effects:                   ;   - Writes a small "digit count"/index nibble to $1FE0 during normalisation.                   ;   - On exit, DE points just past the copied output bytes.                   ;                   ; Open questions / likely dump ambiguities:                   ;   - The many $76 bytes are semantically "wait/consume next token"; confirm against scan.                   ;   - 06AA: 'LD L,$0A' (was transcribed as 2A): it tests buffer[0A] for 05 ('E') like 06A0, then copies the                   ;     exponent sign and digits after it. With 2A the exponent of a number in scientific notation was never printed.                   ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------06BB -----------------------------------------------------------------------------                   ; BURP_PREPARE_16BYTE_OPERAND_BLOCK ($07AC)                   ;  Prepare HL to point at a fixed 16-byte operand/descriptor block.                   ;                   ;  Calling convention:                   ;    IN : A = block selector. Low nibble selects which 16-byte block.                   ;         Bit4 selects base page: 0 -> $1E00, 1 -> $1F00.                   ;    OUT: HL = base + (A & $0F) * 16, where base is $1E00 or $1F00.                   ;    Flags: C is used as a convenient bit4 test (carry after 4x RLA).                   ;                   ;  Implementation notes:                   ;    AND A clears carry. Four RLA operations effectively compute (A << 4)                   ;    and leave carry = original bit4. L receives the shifted value.                   ;    If carry set (original bit4=1) H is incremented to select page $1Fxx.                   ; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------07AC               BURP_BLOCK_ADDR:07AC  A7                   AND A                 ; clear carry; set flags from A 0400–0BFF

FORTH

A small direct-threaded FORTH written for this project to fit where BURP was. The listing is its own source, so the names in threaded definitions link to the words they call. Source: forth/forth.asm.

                   ; COMP80 FORTH                   ;                   ; A small FORTH for the PSI COMP80, in the 2 KB of ROM (0400h-0BFFh) that                   ; held BURP.  The monitor (0000h-03FFh) is unchanged.  As with BURP, the RS                   ; key enters it: the monitor's NMI handler jumps to 0800h.                   ;                   ; Direct-threaded code.  Registers while FORTH runs:                   ;   BC = top of data stack       DE = instruction pointer (IP)                   ;   SP = rest of data stack      IX = return stack pointer                   ;   IY = NEXT (every primitive ends with JP (IY))       HL, A = scratch                   ;                   ; Text is kept in ASCII (20h-5Fh) and converted to the display's 6-bit codes                   ; on output.  The keyboard folds letters to 01h-1Ah; RAWKEY turns them back.                   ;                   ; Keys.  The monitor's NMI handler echoes each key at (DE) and folds codes                   ; 40h and up to 5 bits, so Backspace (08h) and H come out the same.  RS (the ^                   ; key) makes it jump to 0800h; if that happens while FORTH waits in RAWKEY                   ; (the return address on the stack is KEYRET), ENTRY treats it as rubout                   ; instead of a restart.  FS (Ctrl+\) goes back to the monitor.  Any key while                   ; a program runs makes the NMI handler reset to the monitor; RS then comes                   ; back to FORTH with the dictionary kept.                   ;                   ; Screen: line 0 shows FORTH (where the monitor shows READY); lines 1-31                   ; scroll.                   ;                   ; Dictionary header: link (2), length + 80h if immediate (1), name, code.                   ; The user dictionary starts at 0C00h, so TAPE 0C00 <HERE> saves it along                   ; with the three variables in front of it.                    MAGIC:  equ 0C00h           ; MAGICV when the dictionary below is valid                   LATEST: equ 0C02h           ; newest header                   DP:     equ 0C04h           ; HERE                   DICT0:  equ 0C06h           ; first free byte of a new dictionary                   MAGICV: equ 0F04Dh                    TIB:    equ 1D00h           ; input line (80 bytes; page aligned)                   WBUF:   equ 1D50h           ; parsed word, counted (80 bytes)                   CURS:   equ 1DA0h           ; output position in screen memory                   BASE:   equ 1DA2h                   STATE:  equ 1DA4h           ; byte: 0 interpreting, 1 compiling                   TOIN:   equ 1DA6h           ; byte: parse offset into TIB                   NTIB:   equ 1DA7h           ; byte: characters in TIB                   LASTH:  equ 1DA8h           ; newest header made by mkhdr                   R0:     equ 1E40h           ; return stack grows down from here                   S0:     equ 1F00h           ; data stack grows down from here (monitor uses 1Fxx)                    VRAM:   equ 8000h                   CLS64:  equ 0393h           ; monitor: spaces from 8040h to the end of the screen                   PAD64:  equ 03C4h           ; monitor: spaces at (DE) up to the next line                            org 0400h                    ; 0400–0BFF

LISP

A LISP 1.5 style interpreter written for this project, with garbage collection, LAMBDA and dynamic scope, in the same 2 KB. Source: lisp/lisp.asm.

                   ; COMP80 LISP                   ;                   ; A small LISP for the PSI COMP80, in the 2 KB of ROM (0400h-0BFFh) that                   ; held BURP.  The monitor (0000h-03FFh) is unchanged.  As with BURP, the RS                   ; key enters it: the monitor's NMI handler jumps to 0800h.                   ;                   ; Objects are 16-bit words:                   ;   NIL          0                   ;   number n     2n+1 (odd), so -16384..16383                   ;   built-in     2k for built-in function k (below 100h)                   ;   symbol       address of its record in 0C20h-0EFFh: value (2), length                   ;                (1), name; records start on even addresses                   ;   cell         address of a 4-byte cell, car then cdr, in the heap                   ;                (1000h up, or 8800h up with the 32 KB expansion)                   ; so anything from 1000h up that is even is a cell.                   ;                   ; Variables are dynamically scoped with shallow binding: a symbol's value is                   ; in its record, and calling a LAMBDA saves the old values of its parameters                   ; on the binding stack and puts them back when it returns, or when an error                   ; unwinds.  The binding stack grows up from the end of the mark bitmap                   ; (BMEND) and the Z80 stack down from STOP towards it.                   ;                   ; Garbage collection is mark and sweep, with a mark bitmap.  The roots are                   ; the symbol table, the binding stack and the Z80 stack, which is scanned                   ; conservatively: any word on it that could be a cell is taken as one.  So                   ; code only has to keep what it still needs on the stack when it conses.                   ; Free cells have UNB in their car and are chained through their cdr.                   ;                   ; Text is kept in ASCII (20h-5Fh) and converted to the display's 6-bit codes                   ; on output.  Keys and the screen work as in COMP80 FORTH (forth/forth.asm):                   ; RS while waiting for a key is rubout, any key while a program runs resets                   ; to the monitor, and RS from there comes back to LISP with everything kept.                    MAGIC:  equ 0C00h           ; MAGICV when the RAM below holds a LISP world                   SYMEND: equ 0C02h           ; end of the symbol table                   FREE:   equ 0C04h           ; free list                   HEAP:   equ 0C06h           ; first cell        } set at cold start                   HSIZE:  equ 0C08h           ; heap size, bytes  } from MEM16 or MEM48                   BMAP:   equ 0C0Ah           ; mark bitmap       }                   BMEND:  equ 0C0Ch           ; end of the bitmap }                   BSP:    equ 0C0Eh           ; binding stack pointer                   NFREE:  equ 0C10h           ; free cells after the last collection                   CURS:   equ 0C12h           ; output position in screen memory                   TOIN:   equ 0C14h           ; byte: read offset into TIB                   NTIB:   equ 0C15h           ; byte: characters in TIB                   SYMS:   equ 0C20h           ; symbol table, up to SYMTOP                   SYMTOP: equ 0F00h                   TIB:    equ 0F00h           ; input line (80 bytes; page aligned)                   WBUF:   equ 0F50h           ; atom being read, counted                   STOP:   equ 1F00h           ; Z80 stack grows down from here (monitor uses 1Fxx)                   MARGIN: equ 80              ; room kept between the two stacks                   MAGICV: equ 0D15Ah                   UNB:    equ 0FEh            ; value of an unbound symbol; car of a free cell                    NILSYM: equ SYMS            ; the first four symbol records (see NAMES)                   TSYM:   equ SYMS+6                   QUOSYM: equ SYMS+10                   LAMSYM: equ SYMS+18                   FSLIM:  equ 18              ; built-ins below this are special forms                    VRAM:   equ 8000h                   CLS64:  equ 0393h           ; monitor: spaces from 8040h to the end of the screen                   PAD64:  equ 03C4h           ; monitor: spaces at (DE) up to the next line                            org 0400h                    ; ---------------------------------------------------------------- cells                    ; HL = car or cdr of HL; NIL gives NIL. The three 3 KB ROM images, sixteen bytes to a line as in the magazine's listing. The first 400h bytes, the monitor, are the same in all three. Click an address or a byte to see it in the listing. The checksums are the ones the MAME driver expects.
